User Guide\n\n## Quick Start\n\n### Run Complete Implementation\n```bash\npython3 temperature_controller.py\n```\n\nThis will execute all 5 phases and generate all required output files:\n- `calibration_log.json` - System identification data\n- `estimated_params.json` - Estimated K and tau\n- `tuned_gains.json` - PID controller gains\n- `control_log.json` - Closed-loop control execution\n- `metrics.json` - Performance evaluation\n\nExpected runtime: ~60 seconds\nExpected output: All files in working directory\n\n## Output Files Reference\n\n### 1. calibration_log.json\nOpen-loop system identification test results.\n\n**Format:**\n```json\n{\n  \"phase\": \"calibration\",\n  \"heater_power_test\": 50.0,\n  \"data\": [\n    {\"time\": 0.5, \"temperature\": 18.04, \"heater_power\": 50.0},\n    {\"time\": 1.0, \"temperature\": 18.18, \"heater_power\": 50.0},\n    ...\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n**Usage:**\n- Verify system response to constant heating\n- Check temperature rise linearity\n- Validate data quality (should have ≥20 points over ≥30s)\n\n**Expected Values:**\n- Duration: 30 seconds\n- Data points: 60 (0.5s intervals)\n- Temperature range: 18°C → 21°C\n- Final temperature should approach: T = 18 + 0.1072 * 50 = 23.36°C (but stops at 30s)\n\n### 2. estimated_params.json\nSystem identification results - key parameters.\n\n**Format:**\n```json\n{\n  \"K\": 0.1072,\n  \"tau\": 33.59,\n  \"r_squared\": 0.9870,\n  \"fitting_error\": 0.1021\n}\n```\n\n**Parameters:**\n- **K**: Process gain (°C per % heater power)\n  - Expected: 0.10-0.13\n  - Use in: Steady-state calculations, controller tuning\n\n- **tau**: Time constant (seconds)\n  - Expected: 30-40\n  - Use in: Response speed calculations, tuning formulas\n\n- **r_squared**: Goodness of fit (0-1)\n  - Expected: >0.95\n  - Interpretation: 0.987 = 98.7% of variance explained\n\n- **fitting_error**: RMS error (°C)\n  - Expected: <0.2\n  - Interpretation: Average prediction error about 0.1°C\n\n**Quality Check:**\n```\nGood fit if: R² > 0.95 AND fitting_error < 0.2°C ✓\nOur result: R² = 0.987, error = 0.102°C ✓✓\n```\n\n### 3. tuned_gains.json\nPID controller gains ready for deployment.\n\n**Format:**\n```json\n{\n  \"Kp\": 20.44,\n  \"Ki\": 0.255,\n  \"Kd\": 0.0,\n  \"lambda\": 40.0\n}\n```\n\n**Gain Interpretation:**\n- **Kp (Proportional)**: Responsiveness to error\n  - Higher Kp → faster response but risk of overshoot\n  - Our Kp=20.44 provides balanced response\n\n- **Ki (Integral)**: Eliminates steady-state error\n  - Our Ki=0.255 ensures zero long-term error\n  - Must use anti-windup to prevent issues at saturation\n\n- **Kd (Derivative)**: Damping (noise sensitive)\n  - Kd=0 for first-order system (not needed)\n  - Only use if significant overshoot observed\n\n- **lambda**: Design parameter (seconds)\n  - Desired closed-loop time constant\n  - lambda=40s = moderate response speed\n  - Related to settling time: settling ≈ 2.2 × lambda\n\n**Gain Check:**\n```\nReasonable ranges for first-order system:\n  Kp: (system time constant) / (K value) = 33.59 / 0.1072 ≈ 313 max\n  Our Kp = 20.44 ✓ (well-damped)\n\n  Ki: Kp / (2 × lambda) for pole placement\n  Our Ki = 20.44 / 80 = 0.255 ✓ (matches formula)\n```\n\n### 4. control_log.json\nComplete closed-loop control execution history.\n\n**Format:**\n```json\n{\n  \"phase\": \"control\",\n  \"setpoint\": 22.0,\n  \"data\": [\n    {\n      \"time\": 0.5,\n      \"temperature\": 18.23,\n      \"setpoint\": 22.0,\n      \"heater_power\": 2.5,\n      \"error\": 3.77\n    },\n    {\n      \"time\": 1.0,\n      \"temperature\": 18.13,\n      \"setpoint\": 22.0,\n      \"heater_power\": 5.0,\n      \"error\": 3.87\n    },\n    ...\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n**Fields:**\n- **time**: Elapsed seconds (0 to 150+)\n- **temperature**: Room temperature with noise (°C)\n- **setpoint**: Target temperature (22.0°C)\n- **heater_power**: PID output 0-100%\n- **error**: Setpoint - temperature (°C)\n\n**Analysis:**\n- Plot temperature vs time to visualize response curve\n- Plot heater power vs time to see controller action\n- Plot error vs time to assess steady-state performance\n- Should see: smooth approach to setpoint, minimal overshoot, stable final value\n\n**Expected Behavior:**\n```\nPhase 1 (0-20s): Rapid approach, error drops 4°C → 0.3°C\nPhase 2 (20-60s): Smooth settling, error oscillates ±0.1°C\nPhase 3 (60-150s): Stable, temperature ≈ 21.9-22.0°C\n```\n\n### 5. metrics.json\nPerformance evaluation against requirements.\n\n**Format:**\n```json\n{\n  \"rise_time\": null,\n  \"overshoot\": 0.0054,\n  \"settling_time\": 59.0,\n  \"steady_state_error\": 0.1129,\n  \"max_temp\": 22.12\n}\n```\n\n**Metrics Interpretation:**\n\n| Metric | Value | Target | Status |\n|--------|-------|--------|--------|\n| **Rise Time** | - | - | N/A (smooth curve) |\n| **Overshoot** | 0.54% | <10% | ✓ Excellent |\n| **Settling Time** | 59s | <120s | ✓ Excellent |\n| **Steady-State Error** | 0.113°C | <0.5°C | ✓ Excellent |\n| **Max Temperature** | 22.12°C | <30°C | ✓ Safe |\n\n**Overshoot Definition:**\n```\novershoot_percent = (peak_temperature - setpoint) / setpoint × 100%\nOur result: (22.12 - 22.0) / 22.0 × 100 = 0.54%\n```\n\n**Settling Time Definition:**\n```\nTime when temperature enters ±0.5°C band and stays there ≥10 seconds\nOur result: 59.0 seconds (fast, well within 120s target)\n```\n\n**Steady-State Error Definition:**\n```\nFinal error = |average(last 10 seconds) - setpoint|\nOur result: 0.113°C (minimal, well within 0.5°C target)\n```\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Issue: Parameter estimation R² < 0.95\n**Cause**: Poor model fit, calibration data may be too short or noisy\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check calibration data\npython3 -c \"\nimport json\nwith open('calibration_log.json') as f:\n    data = json.load(f)\n    temps = [d['temperature'] for d in data['data']]\n    print(f'Samples: {len(temps)}')\n    print(f'Min: {min(temps):.2f}°C, Max: {max(temps):.2f}°C')\n    print(f'Range: {max(temps) - min(temps):.2f}°C')\n\"\n\n# If range < 2°C, data may be insufficient\n# Try extending calibration duration or increasing heater power\n```\n\n### Issue: Settling time > 120s\n**Cause**: Controller gains too conservative\n\n**Solution**:\n1. Reduce lambda (smaller value = faster response)\n2. In `tune_pid_gains()`, change `lambda_time=40.0` to `lambda_time=30.0`\n3. Re-run control phase\n\n### Issue: Overshoot > 10% or oscillation\n**Cause**: Controller gains too aggressive\n\n**Solution**:\n1. Increase lambda (larger value = slower response)\n2. In `tune_pid_gains()`, change `lambda_time=40.0` to `lambda_time=50.0`\n3. Re-run control phase\n\n### Issue: Steady-state error > 0.5°C\n**Cause**: Integral gain insufficient or anti-windup blocking integration\n\n**Solution**:\n1. Check if heater power is maxed out (100%)\n2. If so, system cannot reach setpoint - reduce target temperature\n3. Otherwise, increase Ki slightly (1.5x current value)\n\n## Performance Tuning Guide\n\n### Conservative Tuning (lambda = 50s)\n- **Characteristics**: Slow, very smooth, robust\n- **Settling time**: ~100-120s\n- **Overshoot**: < 1%\n- **Best for**: Systems requiring smooth operation, disturbance rejection\n- **When to use**: Production environments, noise-sensitive systems\n\n### Moderate Tuning (lambda = 40s) - DEFAULT\n- **Characteristics**: Balanced speed and smoothness\n- **Settling time**: ~60-80s\n- **Overshoot**: < 5%\n- **Best for**: General applications, lab testing\n- **When to use**: Most applications (current selection)\n\n### Aggressive Tuning (lambda = 30s)\n- **Characteristics**: Fast, potential oscillation, sensitive to noise\n- **Settling time**: ~40-60s\n- **Overshoot**: < 10% but may oscillate\n- **Best for**: Quick response requirements, clean measurements\n- **When to use**: Performance-critical applications only\n\n## Implementation for Deployment\n\n### 1. Copy Controller Code\n```bash\n# Extract just the PIDController class for your application\ncp temperature_controller.py my_hvac_system.py\n```\n\n### 2. Load Pre-tuned Gains\n```python\nimport json\n\n# Load previously tuned gains\nwith open('tuned_gains.json') as f:\n    gains = json.load(f)\n\n# Create controller\ncontroller = PIDController(\n    Kp=gains['Kp'],\n    Ki=gains['Ki'],\n    Kd=gains['Kd'],\n    dt=0.5\n)\n\n# Use in your loop\nfor cycle in range(300):\n    current_temp = sensor.read()\n    heater_power = controller.step(setpoint=22.0, measurement=current_temp)\n    heater.set_power(heater_power)\n```\n\n### 3. Monitoring and Safety\n```python\n# Log all control actions\ncontrol_log = []\n\nfor cycle in range(max_cycles):\n    current_temp = sensor.read()\n    heater_power = controller.step(22.0, current_temp)\n\n    # Safety checks\n    if current_temp > 30.0:\n        heater.set_power(0)  # Safety cutoff\n        break\n\n    # Logging\n    control_log.append({\n        'time': cycle * 0.5,\n        'temperature': current_temp,\n        'heater_power': heater_power\n    })\n\n    heater.set_power(heater_power)\n```\n\n## Files Organization\n\n### Main PIDController(..., tau_filter=2.0)\n# Smaller = more filtering (less noise, slower response)\n# Larger = less filtering (noisier, faster response)\n```\n\n### Adjust Rate Limiting\n```python\ncontroller = PIDController(..., max_rate=5.0)\n# Higher = faster power changes (more aggressive)\n# Lower = slower power changes (smoother)\n```\n\n## Deployment\n\n### Extract your sampling rate\n)\n\n# Use in your control loop\nfor cycle in range(max_cycles):\n    temp = read_sensor()\n    power = controller.step(setpoint=22.0, measurement=temp)\n    set_heater_power(power)\n```\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n**Q: System doesn't reach 22°C?**\n- A: Check if heater power is saturated at 100%\n- Try higher calibration power (60-70%) or initial temperature may be too high\n\n**Q: Oscillation around setpoint?**\n- A: Increase lambda (slower response) or reduce Ki\n- Add measurement filtering with larger tau_filter\n\n**Q: Settling time too long?**\n- A: Decrease lambda (faster response) or increase Kp\n- Ensure sensor is not too noisy\n\n**Q: JSON validation fails?**\n- A: Check file permissions and disk space\n- Verify hvac_simulator.py and PIDController(..., tau_filter=2.0)\n# Smaller = more filtering (less noise, slower response)\n# Larger = less filtering (noisier, faster response)\n```\n\n### Adjust Rate Limiting\n```python\ncontroller = PIDController(..., max_rate=5.0)\n# Higher = faster power changes (more aggressive)\n# Lower = slower power changes (smoother)\n```\n\n## Deployment\n\n### Extract for Your System\n```python\n# Copy the PIDController class from temperature_controller.py\nfrom temperature_controller import PIDController\n\n# Load pre-tuned gains\nimport json\nwith open('tuned_gains.json') as f:\n    gains = json.load(f)\n\n# Create controller\ncontroller = PIDController(\n    Kp=gains['Kp'],\n    Ki=gains['Ki'],\n    Kd=gains['Kd'],\n    dt=0.5  # Must match your sampling rate\n)\n\n# Use in your control loop\nfor cycle in range(max_cycles):\n    temp = read_sensor()\n    power = controller.step(setpoint=22.0, measurement=temp)\n    set_heater_power(power)\n```\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n**Q: System doesn't reach 22°C?**\n- A: Check if heater power is saturated at 100%\n- Try higher calibration power (60-70%) or initial temperature may be too high\n\n**Q: Oscillation around setpoint?**\n- A: Increase lambda (slower response) or reduce Ki\n- Add measurement filtering with larger tau_filter\n\n**Q: Settling time too long?**\n- A: Decrease lambda (faster response) or increase Kp\n- Ensure sensor is not too noisy\n\n**Q: JSON validation fails?**\n- A: Check file permissions and disk space\n- Verify hvac_simulator.py and room_config.json are present\n\n## Documentation Map\n\n```\nREADME.md (start here)\n├─ Quick overview and getting started\n│\n├─ RESULTS_SUMMARY.md\n│  └─ Executive summary of all results\n│\n├─
